*   **Novelty:** The idea of query-agnostic KV cache compression is relatively novel.  Most existing approaches are query-aware, limiting their reusability. The use of the LLM itself as an encoder-decoder to determine KV-pair importance via context reconstruction is also a clever idea. It effectively leverages the model's understanding of the context. The application of ideas from self-supervised learning (reconstruction) to this area seems beneficial.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in LLM deployment: memory overhead and latency associated with large KV caches.  KVzip offers a promising solution that significantly reduces these burdens without sacrificing performance. The method has potential benefits for personalized conversational agents, retrieval systems, and other applications where KV caches can be precomputed offline. However, whether its benefits are realized as query length and cache sizes grow remains to be seen.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Query-Agnostic Design:**  The query-agnostic design is a significant advantage, enabling reuse of compressed caches across different queries.
    *   **Context Reconstruction Intuition:** The context reconstruction-based importance scoring is theoretically well-motivated and empirically effective.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The paper presents thorough experimental results on diverse datasets, models, and context lengths, providing strong evidence for the effectiveness of KVzip.
    *   **Scalability Enhancements**: Introduction of chunked scoring helps scale to large context lengths (up to 170k tokens).
    *   **Integration with existing optimizations:** KVzip nicely integrates with existing optimizations like KV cache quantization and structured head-level eviction, showing it complements existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Compression Overhead**: The importance scoring introduces additional overhead (approximately doubling the initial prefill cost). Although this is a one-time cost per context, it's still a consideration, especially for dynamic contexts.
    *   **Chunked Scoring Complexity:** While chunked scoring is necessary for scalability, the dependency across dimensions makes implementation challenging and prevents the direct usage of the flash attention kernel.
    *   **Context Dependence**: The privacy-related behavior change (Table 1) upon using the method warrants further investigation and mitigation strategies.

*   **Impact:** KVzip has the potential to impact LLM deployment by making it more efficient and cost-effective. The code release (linked in the abstract) will facilitate adoption and further research. However, further exploration of how KVzip interacts with other advanced compression techniques is required. Also, more analysis of the privacy implications stemming from the identified privacy behavior will be valuable for adoption.

*   **Justification for the Score:** The paper presents a relatively novel technique with well-motivated design, extensive evaluation, and a promising practical impact. While the overhead of importance scoring and possible privacy behavior change is a limitation, the benefits of query-agnostic compression and integration with other techniques are significant. The weaknesses are reasonable and the method holds potential for further development.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 7/10

### SWE-bench Goes Live!
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23419v1)
- **Authors**: Linghao Zhang, Shilin He, Chaoyun Zhang, Yu Kang, Bowen Li, Chengxing Xie, Junhao Wang, Maoquan Wang, Yufan Huang, Shengyu Fu, Elsie Nallipogu, Qingwei Lin, Yingnong Dang, Saravan Rajmohan, Dongmei Zhang
- **Abstract**: The issue-resolving task, where a model generates patches to fix real-world bugs, has emerged as a critical benchmark for evaluating the capabilities of large language models (LLMs). While SWE-bench and its variants have become standard in this domain, they suffer from key limitations: they have not been updated since their initial releases, cover a narrow set of repositories, and depend heavily on manual effort for instance construction and environment setup. These factors hinder scalability and introduce risks of overfitting and data contamination. In this work, we present \textbf{SWE-bench-Live}, a \textit{live-updatable} benchmark designed to overcome these challenges. Our initial release consists of 1,319 tasks derived from real GitHub issues created since 2024, spanning 93 repositories. Each task is accompanied by a dedicated Docker image to ensure reproducible execution. Central to our benchmark is \method, an automated curation pipeline that streamlines the entire process from instance creation to environment setup, removing manual bottlenecks and enabling scalability and continuous updates. We evaluate a range of state-of-the-art agent frameworks and LLMs on SWE-bench-Live, revealing a substantial performance gap compared to static benchmarks like SWE-bench, even under controlled evaluation conditions. To better understand this discrepancy, we perform detailed analyses across repository origin, issue recency, and task difficulty. By providing a fresh, diverse, and executable benchmark grounded in live repository activity, SWE-bench-Live facilitates rigorous, contamination-resistant evaluation of LLMs and agents in dynamic, real-world software development settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "SWE-bench Goes Live!" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SWE-bench-Live, a dynamically updated benchmark for evaluating large language models (LLMs) in issue resolution tasks.  It addresses limitations of existing static benchmarks like SWE-bench (staleness, limited repository coverage, reliance on manual effort) by automating the entire benchmark creation process. The core component is REPOLAUNCH, an automated curation pipeline that streamlines instance creation and environment setup (using Docker) from real-world GitHub issues. The authors evaluate leading agent frameworks and LLMs on SWE-bench-Live, showing a performance gap compared to SWE-bench.  They analyze this discrepancy based on repository origin, issue recency, and task difficulty.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the **live, automated, and scalable nature** of the benchmark. Existing benchmarks are static and require significant manual effort to maintain and expand. The REPOLAUNCH pipeline automates the entire process from data curation to environment provisioning. This is a significant step forward.  The use of agentic workflows for environment setup is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** SWE-bench-Live addresses a critical problem in LLM evaluation: the risk of data contamination and overfitting on static benchmarks. By providing a continuously updated dataset, the benchmark can help to assess the true generalization capabilities of LLMs in software engineering tasks. The wider repository coverage is also important for the generalization of results.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Automated Pipeline (REPOLAUNCH):** The key technical contribution is the automated pipeline, eliminating manual bottlenecks and enabling continuous updates. This is a major advantage over existing benchmarks.
    *   **Focus on Reproducibility:** The use of Docker ensures reproducibility, a crucial aspect for reliable benchmark results.
    *   **Evaluation of Agent Frameworks:** Evaluating existing LLM-based agents is important to understand the current state-of-the-art and identify areas for improvement.
    *   **Analysis of Performance Discrepancies:** The paper's analysis of why SWE-bench-Live is more challenging than SWE-bench is valuable, revealing limitations of current agents regarding reasoning across files.
    *   **Addressing Data Contamination:** A core strength. The dynamic update addresses concerns about LLMs memorizing training data.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Python-centric:**  The benchmark is currently limited to Python repositories, limiting its scope and generalizability to other programming languages.
    *   **Proxy Metric:**  The reliance on test cases alone to determine if a patch is correct may not fully capture all aspects of issue resolution. There might be cases where the test cases are not comprehensive enough or a correct solution might involve other aspects not captured in tests.
    *   **Limited Agent Evaluation:** While the agents are evaluated, there's a lack of a deep dive into why specific agents struggled.
    *   **Dependency on GitHub Issues:**  The benchmark relies on issues reported in GitHub, which might not be representative of all software maintenance tasks.
    *   **Complexity Heuristics:** The metrics used to proxy task difficulty (file count, lines of code) are quite coarse and might not accurately reflect the actual complexity of the underlying problem.

*   **Potential Influence:** SWE-bench-Live has the potential to become a widely used benchmark for evaluating LLMs in software engineering. Its dynamic nature and reproducibility can drive the development of more robust and generalizable code agents. The open-sourcing of REPOLAUNCH can also enable other researchers to create similar benchmarks for different tasks and domains.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a significant contribution to the field of LLM evaluation in software engineering, it has limitations that prevent it from being considered a truly exceptional (10/10) work. The automated pipeline and focus on live, contamination-resistant benchmarking are important steps forward.  However, the Python-specific scope and the use of test cases as a primary validation method impose some constraints. The heuristic difficulty measures don't represent a thorough, nuanced difficulty assessment. The performance discrepancy analysis could be expanded.

Overall, the paper is well-motivated, technically sound, and addresses a critical issue in the field. It introduces a novel benchmark with the potential to drive progress in LLM-based software engineering.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Enhanced DACER Algorithm with High Diffusion Efficiency
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23426v1)
- **Authors**: Yinuo Wang, Mining Tan, Wenjun Zou, Haotian Lin, Xujie Song, Wenxuan Wang, Tong Liu, Likun Wang, Guojian Zhan, Tianze Zhu, Shiqi Liu, Jingliang Duan, Shengbo Eben Li
- **Abstract**: Due to their expressive capacity, diffusion models have shown great promise in offline RL and imitation learning. Diffusion Actor-Critic with Entropy Regulator (DACER) extended this capability to online RL by using the reverse diffusion process as a policy approximator, trained end-to-end with policy gradient methods, achieving strong performance. However, this comes at the cost of requiring many diffusion steps, which significantly hampers training efficiency, while directly reducing the steps leads to noticeable performance degradation. Critically, the lack of inference efficiency becomes a significant bottleneck for applying diffusion policies in real-time online RL settings. To improve training and inference efficiency while maintaining or even enhancing performance, we propose a Q-gradient field objective as an auxiliary optimization target to guide the denoising process at each diffusion step. Nonetheless, we observe that the independence of the Q-gradient field from the diffusion time step negatively impacts the performance of the diffusion policy. To address this, we introduce a temporal weighting mechanism that enables the model to efficiently eliminate large-scale noise in the early stages and refine actions in the later stages. Experimental results on MuJoCo benchmarks and several multimodal tasks demonstrate that the DACER2 algorithm achieves state-of-the-art performance in most MuJoCo control tasks with only five diffusion steps, while also exhibiting stronger multimodality compared to DACER.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DACER2, an enhancement of the Diffusion Actor-Critic with Entropy Regulator (DACER) algorithm for online reinforcement learning.  DACER2 aims to improve the training and inference efficiency of diffusion policies, which are typically computationally expensive due to the many steps required in the reverse diffusion process. The key innovations in DACER2 are: (1) a Q-gradient field objective used as an auxiliary optimization target to guide the denoising process at each diffusion step, and (2) a temporal weighting mechanism that dynamically adjusts the influence of the Q-gradient field throughout the diffusion process. This temporal weighting allows the model to eliminate large-scale noise early and refine actions later. Experiments on MuJoCo benchmarks and multimodal tasks demonstrate that DACER2 achieves state-of-the-art performance with fewer diffusion steps (5 steps compared to the 20 steps in the original DACER) while maintaining or improving multimodality. The paper also shows that DACER2 has faster training and inference times compared to other diffusion-based RL algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of online reinforcement learning by addressing a key limitation of diffusion policies: their computational cost. The novelty of the work lies in the specific approach to improving efficiency, which combines a Q-gradient field objective with a temporal weighting scheme. While using Q-gradients to guide policy learning isn't entirely novel (e.g., Langevin dynamics), the way it's integrated *specifically within a diffusion policy framework* and with *time-dependent weighting* is a significant and practical improvement. The introduction of the temporal weighting mechanism is well-motivated and addresses a clear shortcoming of directly applying Q-gradients in this context.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies the challenge of computational efficiency in diffusion policies for online RL.
*   **Well-motivated approach:** The Q-gradient field objective and temporal weighting scheme are logically derived and explained.
*   **Strong empirical results:** The experimental evaluation on MuJoCo benchmarks demonstrates state-of-the-art performance and significant improvements in training and inference efficiency. The ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of the proposed techniques.
*   **Practical impact:** Reducing the number of diffusion steps without sacrificing performance has significant implications for real-world applications of diffusion policies in online RL settings.
*  The paper provides a theoretical justification for why Q-value maximization enables the learning of multimodal policies which makes the work stronger.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental novelty:** While the combination of techniques is novel, each individual component (Q-gradients, temporal weighting) has precedents in other areas of RL or diffusion models.
*   **Limited theoretical analysis:**  A deeper theoretical analysis of the convergence properties and optimality of DACER2 would strengthen the paper. While the paper includes a theoretical section, it focuses on justifying the use of a *soft policy* and *maximizing Q-value* rather than the specifics of its Q-gradient based methodology. A convergence analysis using Q-gradient would have greatly strenghened the argument.
*   **Experimental setup:** The paper cites DACER reported results for classical model free baselines. Directly comparing classical model free baselines to DACER2 would strenghen the paper.

**Significance and Impact:**

The paper has a clear potential to influence the field of online RL by making diffusion policies more practical and accessible. The performance improvements and efficiency gains demonstrated by DACER2 could encourage wider adoption of diffusion policies in various RL applications. The method could be extended by improving the stability of Q-value gradient estimation.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper addresses an important problem in online RL and offers a practical solution with solid empirical support. The improvements in training and inference efficiency are significant and have real-world implications. The novel integration of the Q-gradient field objective with a temporal weighting scheme within a diffusion policy framework is a valuable contribution. While the individual components have prior art, the combination and its specific application are novel and effective. The paper provides strong experimental evaluation as well. The areas for improvement are more rigorous convergence/stability analysis and a better experimental comparison with classical baselines.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diversity-Aware Policy Optimization for Large Language Model Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23433v1)
- **Authors**: Jian Yao, Ran Cheng, Xingyu Wu, Jibin Wu, Kay Chen Tan
- **Abstract**: The reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) have advanced rapidly, particularly following the release of DeepSeek R1, which has inspired a surge of research into data quality and reinforcement learning (RL) algorithms. Despite the pivotal role diversity plays in RL, its influence on LLM reasoning remains largely underexplored. To bridge this gap, this work presents a systematic investigation into the impact of diversity in RL-based training for LLM reasoning, and proposes a novel diversity-aware policy optimization method. Across evaluations on 12 LLMs, we observe a strong positive correlation between the solution diversity and Potential at k (a novel metric quantifying an LLM's reasoning potential) in high-performing models. This finding motivates our method to explicitly promote diversity during RL training. Specifically, we design a token-level diversity and reformulate it into a practical objective, then we selectively apply it to positive samples. Integrated into the R1-zero training framework, our method achieves a 3.5 percent average improvement across four mathematical reasoning benchmarks, while generating more diverse and robust solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of solution diversity on the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) during reinforcement learning (RL) based training.  The authors introduce a novel metric called "Potential@k" to quantify an LLM's reasoning potential and empirically demonstrate a positive correlation between solution diversity and Potential@k scores for high-performing LLMs. Motivated by this, they propose a diversity-aware policy optimization method, incorporating a token-level diversity objective that's selectively applied to positive samples.  This method, integrated into the R1-zero framework, achieves performance improvements on mathematical reasoning benchmarks while generating more diverse solutions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty in Problem Formulation:** The paper addresses an important and relatively unexplored aspect of RL for LLMs: the role of diversity.  While diversity is well-known in general RL, its specific impact on LLM reasoning and mathematical problem-solving isn't widely investigated.
*   **Empirical Validation:**  The empirical findings showing the correlation between solution diversity and reasoning potential are compelling. Introducing "Potential@k" provides a valuable tool for analyzing LLM reasoning abilities. The experiments performed across various LLMs increase the credibility of the observations.
*   **Methodological Contribution:** The proposed diversity-aware policy optimization method with a token-level diversity objective, selectively applied to positive samples, offers a practical solution to enhance diversity without sacrificing training stability. The justification based on gradient behavior analysis provides additional insight into the method's effectiveness.
*   **Clear Experimental Results:** The experimental results on mathematical reasoning benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method compared to the standard R1-zero training.  The qualitative analysis showcases the generation of diverse solutions for the same problem.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Constraints:** The experiments are conducted on relatively small-scale models (7B and 1.5B parameters) due to computational limitations. The authors acknowledge that the generalizability to larger models requires further investigation. This limits the immediate impact of the research.
*   **Limited Dataset:** Training and evaluation primarily focuses on the GSM8K dataset and variations. While GSM8K is a common benchmark, the analysis should include a wide array of datasets.
*   **Diversity metric**: The adopted equation diversity (Div-Equ) from prior work is not well-defined and may be too simple to measure the variety of LLM solutions.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to a better understanding of the factors influencing LLM reasoning capabilities. It provides empirical evidence supporting the importance of solution diversity and proposes a method to promote diversity during RL-based LLM training. While the scope is somewhat limited by the model sizes and dataset choice, the findings offer valuable insights for future research in RL for LLMs.  The work has the potential to influence the design of more robust and creative LLMs for reasoning tasks.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents novel findings regarding the role of diversity in LLM reasoning and introduces a practical method to promote diversity during training. The empirical evaluation, though limited by computational resources, supports the claims. Therefore, based on the above evaluation, it is justified to assigned a rating of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### CryoCCD: Conditional Cycle-consistent Diffusion with Biophysical Modeling for Cryo-EM Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23444v1)
- **Authors**: Runmin Jiang, Genpei Zhang, Yuntian Yang, Siqi Wu, Yuheng Zhang, Wanyue Feng, Yizhou Zhao, Xi Xiao, Xiao Wang, Tianyang Wang, Xingjian Li, Min Xu
- **Abstract**: Cryo-electron microscopy (cryo-EM) offers near-atomic resolution imaging of macromolecules, but developing robust models for downstream analysis is hindered by the scarcity of high-quality annotated data. While synthetic data generation has emerged as a potential solution, existing methods often fail to capture both the structural diversity of biological specimens and the complex, spatially varying noise inherent in cryo-EM imaging. To overcome these limitations, we propose CryoCCD, a synthesis framework that integrates biophysical modeling with generative techniques. Specifically, CryoCCD produces multi-scale cryo-EM micrographs that reflect realistic biophysical variability through compositional heterogeneity, cellular context, and physics-informed imaging. To generate realistic noise, we employ a conditional diffusion model, enhanced by cycle consistency to preserve structural fidelity and mask-aware contrastive learning to capture spatially adaptive noise patterns. Extensive experiments show that CryoCCD generates structurally accurate micrographs and enhances performance in downstream tasks, outperforming state-of-the-art baselines in both particle picking and reconstruction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CryoCCD paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CryoCCD, a novel framework for synthesizing realistic cryo-EM micrographs. It addresses limitations of existing methods by combining biophysical modeling with a conditional cycle-consistent diffusion model.  CryoCCD generates multi-scale micrographs reflecting biophysical variability (compositional heterogeneity, cellular context) and complex, spatially varying noise.  The diffusion model is enhanced with cycle consistency for structural fidelity and mask-aware contrastive learning for capturing spatially adaptive noise. Experimental results demonstrate CryoCCD's superior performance in downstream tasks like particle picking and 3D reconstruction compared to state-of-the-art baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The combination of biophysical simulation with diffusion models is a significant step forward in cryo-EM data synthesis.  Prior works either relied on less realistic noise models (Gaussian-based) or GAN-based approaches that lack the stability and structural fidelity of diffusion models.  Cycle consistency and mask-guided contrastive learning are clever additions to improve the realism and controllability of the synthetic data. The most novel aspect is the integration of diffusion models, previously underexplored in this domain, within a biophysical modeling pipeline.

*   **Significance:**  The generation of high-quality synthetic cryo-EM data addresses a crucial need in the field: the scarcity of annotated data. This bottleneck hinders the development of robust machine learning algorithms for particle picking, pose estimation, and 3D reconstruction. By providing a way to generate realistic and controllable data, CryoCCD has the potential to accelerate algorithm development and improve the accuracy of structural determination pipelines. Improved performance on downstream tasks like particle picking translates directly to reduced human annotation effort and potentially higher resolution reconstructions.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Realistic Noise Modeling:** Addresses a significant limitation of existing biophysical simulators. The conditional diffusion model appears to capture the complex noise patterns better than Gaussian-based approaches.
    *   **Structural Fidelity:** Cycle consistency helps preserve structural details during noise synthesis, a key advantage over purely generative approaches that might distort or lose structural information.
    *   **Controllability:** Mask-guided contrastive learning provides control over noise synthesis, allowing researchers to target specific regions or features for enhancement.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents thorough experimental results, including visual comparisons, quantitative metrics (FID, AUPRC, resolution), and ablation studies. Demonstrates practical utility in downstream tasks.
    *   **Biophysical Foundation:** The use of a well-defined biophysical model provides a strong prior for data generation, ensuring that the synthetic data is physically plausible.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Mask Dependence:** While mask-guided contrastive learning is a strength, the dependence on masks as conditional inputs could also be a limitation. Generating accurate masks for real cryo-EM data can be challenging. The authors acknowledge this in the Limitation Discussion section. The quality of the final result heavily depends on the quality of the mask input.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models can be computationally expensive to train and sample from. The paper doesn't explicitly address the computational cost of CryoCCD compared to other synthesis methods. While DPM-Solver is used for acceleration, it would be useful to have a direct comparison of runtime.
    *   **Limited Validation on complex, flexible structures:** The downstream tasks and evaluations are focused on somewhat rigid particles. More work is needed to understand the improvements on highly dynamic proteins.

*   **Potential Influence:** CryoCCD has the potential to significantly impact the field by:

    *   Enabling the development of more robust and accurate machine learning algorithms for cryo-EM data analysis.
    *   Reducing the reliance on manually annotated data, which is time-consuming and expensive.
    *   Facilitating the training of algorithms on specific types of structures or imaging conditions.
    *   Providing a tool for simulating and understanding the effects of noise on cryo-EM data.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach to cryo-EM data synthesis with solid theoretical underpinnings and demonstrably better performance than existing methods in critical downstream tasks. The integration of biophysical modeling and diffusion models is a creative and impactful combination. However, the mask dependency is a potential limitation, and more information on the computational cost would be helpful. The performance gain is solid, but still leaves significant room for improvements. Therefore, while a significant advance, the practical impacts remain to be seen.

### CMIE: Combining MLLM Insights with External Evidence for Explainable Out-of-Context Misinformation Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23449v1)
- **Authors**: Fanxiao Li, Jiaying Wu, Canyuan He, Wei Zhou
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities in visual reasoning and text generation. While previous studies have explored the application of MLLM for detecting out-of-context (OOC) misinformation, our empirical analysis reveals two persisting challenges of this paradigm. Evaluating the representative GPT-4o model on direct reasoning and evidence augmented reasoning, results indicate that MLLM struggle to capture the deeper relationships-specifically, cases in which the image and text are not directly connected but are associated through underlying semantic links. Moreover, noise in the evidence further impairs detection accuracy. To address these challenges, we propose CMIE, a novel OOC misinformation detection framework that incorporates a Coexistence Relationship Generation (CRG) strategy and an Association Scoring (AS) mechanism. CMIE identifies the underlying coexistence relationships between images and text, and selectively utilizes relevant evidence to enhance misinformation detection. Experimental results demonstrate that our approach outperforms existing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of detecting out-of-context (OOC) misinformation in multimodal data (images and text) using Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). It identifies limitations in existing approaches, particularly the reliance on superficial correlations and the sensitivity to noisy evidence. The authors propose a novel framework called CMIE (Combining MLLM Insights with External Evidence), which incorporates a Coexistence Relationship Generation (CRG) strategy to identify underlying relationships between images and text, and an Association Scoring (AS) mechanism to selectively utilize relevant external evidence. The authors empirically evaluate CMIE against several baselines and conduct ablation studies demonstrating the effectiveness of their approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the combination of CRG and AS to improve the utilization of external evidence for OOC misinformation detection. While RAG-based approaches are not new, CMIE attempts to provide a more sophisticated method for filtering and incorporating external information. The idea of generating "coexistence relationships" is interesting, although the implementation relies on the MLLM's internal knowledge, which could be a limitation.

*   **Significance:** The problem of multimodal misinformation detection is significant, and improved methods for identifying and explaining OOC content are valuable. CMIE aims to provide more human-readable explanations and improved accuracy. While the empirical results suggest improvements over some baselines, the impact of these improvements is not necessarily dramatic or transformative.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a practically relevant problem.
    *   Provides a clear problem formulation.
    *   Presents a novel framework (CMIE) with two key components (CRG and AS).
    *   Empirically evaluates CMIE against competitive baselines.
    *   Performs ablation studies to validate the design choices.
    *   Includes a qualitative case study.
    *   Attempts to provide more explainable results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on MLLM's Internal Knowledge:** The core of CMIE depends on the MLLM's ability to generate reasonable coexistence relationships and association scores. This raises concerns about the model's potential biases, hallucinations, or limitations in capturing subtle semantic links. The study does mention attempts to alleviate prompt sensitivities, but the inherent reliability of MLLM outputs still needs further investigation.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** While the results indicate improvements over some baselines, the gains are not exceptionally large. The SNIFFER baseline also relies on MLLMs for explanations generation and yields better results, indicating potential upper-bounds on performance for MLLM techniques.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** The paper lacks in-depth theoretical justification for the design choices of the CRG and AS components. While the ablation studies provide some evidence, a more rigorous mathematical or information-theoretic analysis could strengthen the framework.
    *   **Generalizability:** The experiments are conducted on a single dataset (NewsCLIPpings). While this is a common benchmark, further evaluation on other datasets would be necessary to assess the generalizability of CMIE to different domains and modalities.

*   **Potential Influence:** The idea of extracting and scoring relationships between image and text could be influential in the field. CMIE may inspire other researchers to explore more sophisticated methods for integrating external knowledge into MLLMs.
*   **Missing Details:** the prompt-engineering techniques that the paper relies on are not entirely clear, which would limit replication efforts.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-motivated framework for OOC misinformation detection. It proposes a novel combination of techniques (CRG and AS) to improve the utilization of external evidence and provides empirical evidence supporting its effectiveness. However, the reliance on MLLM's internal knowledge, the incremental improvements, and the limited theoretical justification and generalizability concerns temper the overall significance of the contribution.

### What About Emotions? Guiding Fine-Grained Emotion Extraction from Mobile App Reviews
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23452v1)
- **Authors**: Quim Motger, Marc Oriol, Max Tiessler, Xavier Franch, Jordi Marco
- **Abstract**: Opinion mining plays a vital role in analysing user feedback and extracting insights from textual data. While most research focuses on sentiment polarity (e.g., positive, negative, neutral), fine-grained emotion classification in app reviews remains underexplored. This paper addresses this gap by identifying and addressing the challenges and limitations in fine-grained emotion analysis in the context of app reviews. Our study adapts Plutchik's emotion taxonomy to app reviews by developing a structured annotation framework and dataset. Through an iterative human annotation process, we define clear annotation guidelines and document key challenges in emotion classification. Additionally, we evaluate the feasibility of automating emotion annotation using large language models, assessing their cost-effectiveness and agreement with human-labelled data. Our findings reveal that while large language models significantly reduce manual effort and maintain substantial agreement with human annotators, full automation remains challenging due to the complexity of emotional interpretation. This work contributes to opinion mining by providing structured guidelines, an annotated dataset, and insights for developing automated pipelines to capture the complexity of emotions in app reviews.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the application of fine-grained emotion extraction to mobile app reviews, addressing a gap in sentiment analysis research that typically focuses on polarity (positive/negative/neutral). The authors adapt Plutchik's emotion taxonomy to the context of app reviews, creating a structured annotation framework and dataset. They present annotation guidelines, an annotated dataset of 1,112 app review sentences, and insights into the challenges of human emotion annotation. The paper further evaluates the feasibility of using Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate emotion annotation, assessing their cost-effectiveness and agreement with human-labeled data. Findings indicate that while LLMs can significantly reduce manual effort and maintain reasonable agreement, full automation remains difficult due to the complexity of emotional interpretation. The research provides structured guidelines, an annotated dataset, and recommendations for developing automated pipelines for capturing emotions in app reviews.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The novelty lies in several aspects:

*   **Adaptation of Emotion Taxonomy:** While emotion taxonomies exist, their application to *app review* data, specifically with a focus on adapting Plutchik's model in this domain, presents a unique contribution. The annotation scheme is specifically tailored to app reviews and the language users employ when reviewing apps.
*   **Structured Annotation Process & Guidelines:**  The iterative annotation process, resulting in a structured set of guidelines and addressing key challenges in emotion classification *within the app review context*, is valuable.  Many opinion mining studies lack this level of detail regarding the annotation process. The paper provides detailed guidelines that others can build upon and use.
*   **Comparative Analysis of LLMs:** The assessment of LLMs for automated emotion annotation, evaluating both cost-efficiency and agreement with human annotators, adds to the growing body of research on using LLMs for software engineering tasks.

**Significance:**

The significance is based on the following arguments:

*   **Addressing Granularity Limitations:** Polarity-based sentiment analysis is limited; fine-grained emotion analysis can provide richer insights into user feedback, supporting more informed decision-making in requirements engineering (RE). The examples of nuanced insights derived from different emotions are convincing.
*   **Publicly Available Resources:** The open sharing of the annotated dataset and source code is significant. This allows other researchers to build upon the work and validate the findings, further advancing the field.
*   **Practical Guidance:** The paper's detailed discussion of challenges in emotion annotation and the design suggestions for automated methods offers valuable practical guidance for researchers and practitioners working in this area. This reduces the burden on others to create their dataset and taxonomy from scratch.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Domain Specificity:**  While the focus on app reviews provides a clear context, it also limits the generalizability of the findings to other types of software reviews or user feedback.
*   **Annotation Agreement:** Although the inter-annotator agreement reached a substantial level, it's still not perfect. It needs to be acknowledged that perfect annotation is nearly impossible, and this study shows a robust process.
*   **LLM Evaluation Depth:** While the LLM evaluation is good, a deeper dive into the types of errors made by LLMs (e.g., confusion matrices at the emotion level) could have provided more actionable insights for improving automated emotion extraction.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a valuable contribution by addressing a specific gap in the field of sentiment analysis by applying emotion classification to app reviews. The detailed annotation process, the creation of a publicly available dataset, and the comparison of LLMs contribute to the existing body of knowledge. The paper also details design considerations for the automated classification of emotions. However, the somewhat limited generalizability (due to the specific focus on app reviews) and certain limitations in the LLM evaluation prevent it from achieving a higher score. The paper is well-written, rigorous in its methodology, and presents findings that have potential to influence future research in software engineering and requirements engineering.

### Diffusion Guidance Is a Controllable Policy Improvement Operator
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23458v1)
- **Authors**: Kevin Frans, Seohong Park, Pieter Abbeel, Sergey Levine
- **Abstract**: At the core of reinforcement learning is the idea of learning beyond the performance in the data. However, scaling such systems has proven notoriously tricky. In contrast, techniques from generative modeling have proven remarkably scalable and are simple to train. In this work, we combine these strengths, by deriving a direct relation between policy improvement and guidance of diffusion models. The resulting framework, CFGRL, is trained with the simplicity of supervised learning, yet can further improve on the policies in the data. On offline RL tasks, we observe a reliable trend -- increased guidance weighting leads to increased performance. Of particular importance, CFGRL can operate without explicitly learning a value function, allowing us to generalize simple supervised methods (e.g., goal-conditioned behavioral cloning) to further prioritize optimality, gaining performance for "free" across the board.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Diffusion Guidance Is a Controllable Policy Improvement Operator":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CFGRL, a novel reinforcement learning framework that leverages the power of diffusion model guidance for controllable policy improvement. It establishes a theoretical connection between diffusion model guidance and policy improvement in RL, framing policy optimization as a product of a prior reference policy and an "optimality" distribution, conditioned on advantage. CFGRL is trained with the simplicity of behavioral cloning yet can improve upon the policies present in the data. Critically, it doesn't always require an explicit value function, allowing it to augment simpler supervised methods like goal-conditioned behavioral cloning. Experiments demonstrate CFGRL's effectiveness in both offline RL and goal-conditioned settings, showcasing improved performance compared to standard weighted regression and behavioral cloning techniques. A key feature of CFGRL is that the level of policy improvement can be controlled at test time using the guidance weight, offering flexibility without retraining.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of connecting diffusion guidance to policy improvement in RL, especially without explicitly learning a value function in some cases, is a significant contribution. This bridge between generative modeling and RL is a promising avenue for research. Prior work leveraged diffusion models for RL, but typically focused on extracting a policy to maximize the Q-function using methods like weighted regression, reparameterization, or rejection sampling. CFGRL presents a novel approach using classifier-free guidance as a policy extraction mechanism which has several benefits over existing methods.
    The notion of representing policies as products of a reference policy and an optimality distribution, then using diffusion guidance to sample from this product, seems novel. However, the paper needs to better situate itself against concurrent work, as indicated in the related works section. Specifically, its distinction over Kuba et al [40] and Chen et al [9] are valuable but not entirely convincing given the proximity in objectives. The paper also presents connections between its work and iterative, KL-constrained methods which increase the novelty of this work.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in RL: scaling up algorithms while maintaining training stability. By utilizing the well-established and scalable diffusion model training process, CFGRL offers a practical approach to improving policies, particularly in offline settings where data may be suboptimal. The "free" performance gains in goal-conditioned settings, where value function learning is often avoided for simplicity, are especially significant. The controllable policy improvement is a key advantage of CFGRL and provides practical advantages over methods such as weighting techniques.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Theoretical Foundation:** The paper is grounded in a formal connection between diffusion guidance and policy improvement, which enhances its credibility and provides insights into its behavior. The proofs in the appendix further strengthens this foundation.
    *   **Practical Advantages:** The ability to control the degree of policy improvement at test time is a significant advantage, as it avoids the need for extensive hyperparameter tuning during training. The value-function-free capability is valuable for scaling to more complex tasks.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of CFGRL in a variety of settings and convincingly show improved performance over relevant baselines. The ablation study is also informative.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Clarity:** While the core concepts are sound, the presentation could be improved to make the ideas more accessible. The connection to previous work such as [40] could be more fleshed out.
    *   **Generality of theoretical framework:** While there is value in framing the optimization as a product of two factors, it may not necessarily be a fundamentally more general form of expressing any policy.
    *   **Limited scope of comparison:** The paper mainly focuses on comparing CFGRL to weighted regression and behavioral cloning methods, but it does not extensively compare it to more advanced policy extraction algorithms or other diffusion model policy learning methods. A discussion on the limitations and trade-offs involved is missing.

*   **Potential Influence:** CFGRL has the potential to influence the field by providing a new way to integrate generative modeling techniques into RL. Its simplicity and scalability make it attractive for practical applications. It also opens up new research directions in controllable policy improvement and value-function-free RL.

**Justification of Score:**

While the theoretical connection established and the ability to perform controllable policy improvement are strengths, the paper's novelty is slightly limited by its similarity to some concurrent works. The empirical evaluation, while promising, could be strengthened by comparing against a broader set of baselines. I believe that the paper could still provide a novel approach to a previously unexplored area, but requires further validation. Therefore I will assign a score that reflects this.

### LAFR: Efficient Diffusion-based Blind Face Restoration via Latent Codebook Alignment Adapter
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23462v1)
- **Authors**: Runyi Li, Bin Chen, Jian Zhang, Radu Timofte
- **Abstract**: Blind face restoration from low-quality (LQ) images is a challenging task that requires not only high-fidelity image reconstruction but also the preservation of facial identity. While diffusion models like Stable Diffusion have shown promise in generating high-quality (HQ) images, their VAE modules are typically trained only on HQ data, resulting in semantic misalignment when encoding LQ inputs. This mismatch significantly weakens the effectiveness of LQ conditions during the denoising process. Existing approaches often tackle this issue by retraining the VAE encoder, which is computationally expensive and memory-intensive. To address this limitation efficiently, we propose LAFR (Latent Alignment for Face Restoration), a novel codebook-based latent space adapter that aligns the latent distribution of LQ images with that of HQ counterparts, enabling semantically consistent diffusion sampling without altering the original VAE. To further enhance identity preservation, we introduce a multi-level restoration loss that combines constraints from identity embeddings and facial structural priors. Additionally, by leveraging the inherent structural regularity of facial images, we show that lightweight finetuning of diffusion prior on just 0.9% of FFHQ dataset is sufficient to achieve results comparable to state-of-the-art methods, reduce training time by 70%. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world face restoration benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of LAFR, achieving high-quality, identity-preserving face reconstruction from severely degraded inputs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "LAFR: Efficient Diffusion-based Blind Face Restoration via Latent Codebook Alignment Adapter":

**Summary:**

The paper presents LAFR, a method for efficient blind face restoration using diffusion models. The core idea is to address the semantic misalignment between low-quality (LQ) and high-quality (HQ) images in the latent space of a pre-trained VAE. Instead of retraining the entire VAE, the authors propose a lightweight codebook-based latent space adapter to align the latent distributions of LQ images with their HQ counterparts. This alignment facilitates semantically consistent diffusion sampling. Further, they introduce a multi-level restoration loss combining identity embeddings and facial structure priors to enhance identity preservation.  The method fine-tunes the diffusion prior on a small subset of FFHQ, achieving performance comparable to state-of-the-art methods with reduced training time. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness and efficiency of LAFR through experiments on synthetic and real-world face restoration benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the efficient adaptation strategy for diffusion-based face restoration. While latent space alignment and multi-level loss functions are not entirely new concepts, the specific combination of a *lightweight codebook-based adapter* *paired with extremely limited fine-tuning* is a significant contribution. The adapter design focusing on aligning LQ latents to the pretrained HQ VAE latent space is a clever approach, avoiding the computational overhead of retraining the VAE or using complex alignment modules as in previous works. Claiming an adaptation strategy needing only ~0.9% of the FFHQ dataset is a compelling advantage if properly substantiated.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is potentially high, as it addresses a critical bottleneck in diffusion-based face restoration: the computational cost of adapting pre-trained models for specific tasks. The ability to achieve competitive results with a significantly reduced training dataset and parameter count has several important implications:
    *   **Accessibility:** Makes high-quality face restoration more accessible to researchers and practitioners with limited computational resources.
    *   **Efficiency:** Reduces the environmental impact and energy consumption associated with training large models.
    *   **Adaptability:** Facilitates faster adaptation of pre-trained diffusion models to new datasets and restoration tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Efficient adaptation:** The codebook-based adapter and limited fine-tuning strategy are effective and efficient, as demonstrated by the experimental results.
    *   **Identity preservation:** The multi-level restoration loss contributes to maintaining facial identity during restoration.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation on diverse benchmarks, including both synthetic and real-world datasets. Quantitative metrics and qualitative visualizations are provided.
    *   **Clear and well-written:** The paper is well-structured and easy to follow.
    *   **Detailed Ablation studies:** Strong ablation study to validate the architecture design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** The approach relies on a pre-trained Stable Diffusion model. While this is a common practice, the method's performance may be limited by the capabilities of the underlying pre-trained model.
    *   **Limitations discussion:** It lacks further discussion on the types of degradations the method handles well or poorly. Could the approach struggle when there is significant occlusion, extreme poses, or non-standard facial features?
    *   **Codebook learning discussion**: It could benefit from adding more explanation of the details on the codebook generation process, such as the size of the codebook, the feature used to query for nearest neighbors.
    *   **Potential for bias**: The model is trained primarily using FFHQ and CelebA-HQ datasets. Face restoration systems can exhibit racial or ethnic biases. The study should explore if such bias is present.

*   **Justification of Performance Claims:** The comparisons to state-of-the-art methods appear to be fair, although re-training certain baselines on FFHQ gives them an inherent advantage that LAFR aims to avoid. The detailed ablation study provides solid support to the importance of each component of the design.

*   **Potential influence on the field:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in diffusion-based image restoration by highlighting the importance of efficient adaptation strategies. If the findings are widely adopted, they could lead to more accessible and sustainable face restoration technologies.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of blind face restoration. The core idea of a lightweight codebook-based adapter for latent space alignment is both novel and impactful. While individual components are not entirely new, their strategic combination coupled with very limited training on a small subset of FFHQ makes LAFR stand out for its efficiency. However, the reliance on a pre-trained model, the potential for bias, and the limited discussion of failure cases slightly temper the enthusiasm. Overall, the paper presents a solid approach with significant practical benefits, justifying a score of 7. The reduction in training costs, coupled with competitive performance and a detailed ablation study, firmly place it as a noteworthy contribution that deserves attention from the research community.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Synthesizing Performance Constraints for Evaluating and Improving Code Efficiency
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23471v1)
- **Authors**: Jun Yang, Cheng-Chi Wang, Bogdan Alexandru Stoica, Kexin Pei
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have been increasingly used to optimize code efficiency. Evaluating their effectiveness and further suggesting optimization opportunities often rely on high-quality tests to demonstrate the performance bottlenecks presented in the program. However, existing approaches rely on a limited set of hand-curated inputs or LLM-generated uninteresting length-stressing tests, failing to reveal more nuanced optimization opportunities. We present WEDGE, a framework for generating performance-stressing input given the program under test. WEDGE synthesizes explicit performance-characterizing constraints in the form of branch conditions to partition the programs' execution space into performance-specific regions. When integrated with the coverage-guided fuzzer, reaching different regions introduces explicit rewards for test generation to explore inefficient implementations. Our evaluation shows that WEDGE introduces a significant slowdown compared to the tests in CodeContests and those claimed to be optimized by existing approaches. From the utility perspective, integrating our tests substantially improves the existing code optimization approaches that rely on test-driven execution feedback. We release PERFFORGE, the performance tests generated by WEDGE, to benchmark future approaches for efficient code generation at https://github.com/UChiSeclab/perfforge.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Synthesizing Performance Constraints for Evaluating and Improving Code Efficiency":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WEDGE, a novel framework designed to generate performance-stressing test inputs for programs, specifically targeting code optimization efforts driven by Large Language Models (LLMs). WEDGE departs from existing approaches that rely on limited hand-curated inputs or generic length-stressing tests. Instead, it synthesizes explicit performance-characterizing constraints. This involves: (1) profiling code execution to identify "fast" and "slow" input pairs; (2) leveraging LLMs to infer performance-characterizing constraints based on the contrasting profiles and instrument the code with checkers; and (3) utilizing a coverage-guided fuzzer to generate inputs that satisfy these constraints, effectively exploring inefficient implementations. The paper demonstrates that WEDGE generates significantly more performance-stressing tests than existing methods, which can subsequently improve code optimization techniques that rely on execution feedback. They also release a performance testing suite (PerfForge).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the integration of several existing techniques in a unique and targeted manner. While LLMs, fuzzing, and performance profiling are individually well-established, their combination to generate performance-stressing tests is innovative. The key contribution is *how* they are combined: using LLMs to infer *local* performance constraints and leveraging fuzzing for efficient search, rather than relying solely on LLMs for end-to-end input generation. This decomposition of the problem is a crucial step that mitigates the limitations of LLMs in global reasoning about performance. Another strength is their careful use of constraint-aware fuzzing, and the overall workflow is quite sound.
*   **Significance:** The work addresses a critical challenge in LLM-based code optimization: the lack of high-quality performance tests. The paper shows demonstrably that current correctness tests are inadequate for revealing performance bottlenecks. WEDGE's ability to generate more performance-stressing inputs directly improves the efficacy of LLM-based optimization techniques, making a tangible contribution to the field. It also provides a benchmark (PerfForge) which can be used to evaluate future optimization approaches. This enables better evaluation and comparison of various optimization strategies.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides extensive experimental validation demonstrating the superiority of WEDGE-generated tests over baselines. The utility analysis with Effi-Learner and PIE further reinforces the practical benefits of the approach.
    *   **Well-Defined Problem Decomposition:** Decomposing the problem into performance characterization via LLMs and constraint-guided fuzzing is a key architectural strength. This avoids relying on a single method for everything.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contributions of different components of the framework.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The case studies offer valuable insights into the types of performance bottlenecks that WEDGE is able to uncover and why it outperforms other techniques.
    *   **Artifact Release:** Releasing PERFFORGE promotes reproducible research and serves as a valuable resource for the community.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability (Potential):**  While the evaluation covers a decent range of CodeContests problems, there is an inherit limitation in the evaluation and the ability of the workflow to adapt to extremely complicated or resource intensive programs.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The reliance on LLMs introduces several dependencies and potential limitations.  The quality of the synthesized constraints depends on the LLM's reasoning abilities. Also, the system is inherently tied to the capabilities and biases of the LLMs. The prompt engineering can also be fragile.
    *   **Heuristic Thresholds and Parameters:** The framework relies on several heuristic thresholds, e.g., for determining fast vs. slow inputs. The sensitivity of the performance to these choices is not fully explored (although they do provide ablation studies).
    *   **Runtime Overhead:** The performance profiling and fuzzing steps introduce considerable runtime overhead, limiting the scalability of the approach. Though they're careful to measure CPU instructions, the initial overhead for profiling may make this difficult to apply to larger programs.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has good potential to influence future research in LLM-based code optimization, test generation, and fuzzing. It highlights the limitations of current testing methods and provides a promising direction for generating more effective performance tests.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM-based code optimization. The innovative combination of LLMs for constraint synthesis with coverage-guided fuzzing is a valuable insight. The strong empirical results and released benchmark further strengthen the impact. While there are limitations related to the LLM dependency, heuristic thresholds, and potential scalability issues, the overall impact is strong.

### EVOREFUSE: Evolutionary Prompt Optimization for Evaluation and Mitigation of LLM Over-Refusal to Pseudo-Malicious Instructions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23473v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaorui Wu, Xiaofeng Mao, Fei Li, Xin Zhang, Xiaolu Zhang, Jun Zhou, Yuxiang Peng, Li Zheng, Chong Teng, Donghong Ji, Zhuang Li
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) frequently refuse to respond to pseudo-malicious instructions: semantically harmless input queries triggering unnecessary LLM refusals due to conservative safety alignment, significantly impairing user experience. Collecting such instructions is crucial for evaluating and mitigating over-refusals, but existing instruction curation methods, like manual creation or instruction rewriting, either lack scalability or fail to produce sufficiently diverse and effective refusal-inducing prompts. To address these limitations, we introduce EVOREFUSE, a prompt optimization approach that generates diverse pseudo-malicious instructions consistently eliciting confident refusals across LLMs. EVOREFUSE employs an evolutionary algorithm exploring the instruction space in more diverse directions than existing methods via mutation strategies and recombination, and iteratively evolves seed instructions to maximize evidence lower bound on LLM refusal probability. Using EVOREFUSE, we create two novel datasets: EVOREFUSE-TEST, a benchmark of 582 pseudo-malicious instructions that outperforms the next-best benchmark with 140.41% higher average refusal triggering rate across 9 LLMs, 34.86% greater lexical diversity, and 40.03% improved LLM response confidence scores; and EVOREFUSE-ALIGN, which provides 3,000 pseudo-malicious instructions with responses for supervised and preference-based alignment training. LLAMA3.1-8B-INSTRUCT supervisedly fine-tuned on EVOREFUSE-ALIGN achieves up to 14.31% fewer over-refusals than models trained on the second-best alignment dataset, without compromising safety. Our analysis with EVOREFUSE-TEST reveals models trigger over-refusals by overly focusing on sensitive keywords while ignoring broader context.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "EVOREFUSE: Evolutionary Prompt Optimization for Evaluation and Mitigation of LLM Over-Refusal to Pseudo-Malicious Instructions" addresses the problem of LLMs unnecessarily refusing to respond to harmless user inputs (over-refusal). The authors introduce EVOREFUSE, an evolutionary algorithm that generates diverse "pseudo-malicious" instructions designed to consistently trigger refusals. EVOREFUSE uses an Evidence Lower Bound (ELBO) as a fitness function to balance instruction diversity and refusal-inducing capabilities. The paper presents two novel datasets created using EVOREFUSE: EVOREFUSE-TEST (a benchmark for evaluating over-refusal) and EVOREFUSE-ALIGN (a dataset for mitigating over-refusal through alignment training).  Experiments demonstrate that the generated datasets are more challenging and effective for evaluating and mitigating over-refusal compared to existing methods. The paper also analyzes the causes of over-refusal, attributing it to shortcut learning and the influence of early transformer layers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel approach (EVOREFUSE) to automatically generate prompts that expose over-refusal behavior in LLMs. The use of an evolutionary algorithm with an ELBO-based fitness function is a significant contribution. The creation of two new datasets, EVOREFUSE-TEST and EVOREFUSE-ALIGN, provides valuable resources for the research community. The insights into the causes of over-refusal (shortcut learning and early-layer biases) are also valuable, though not entirely groundbreaking, confirming existing hypotheses in the field. Prior work exists on adversarial prompt generation and safety evaluation, but EVOREFUSE's targeted focus on over-refusal, combined with its evolutionary approach and downstream alignment task, distinguishes it.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem: over-refusal in LLMs, which negatively impacts user experience and model helpfulness.  By providing a benchmark (EVOREFUSE-TEST) and a training dataset (EVOREFUSE-ALIGN), the authors enable more robust evaluation and effective mitigation strategies. Demonstrating improvements in over-refusal reduction through alignment training is a practically significant result. The insights into the mechanisms underlying over-refusal (shortcut learning, influence of early transformer layers) contribute to a better understanding of LLM behavior and can inform future safety alignment strategies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The evolutionary algorithm with ELBO-based fitness function is a well-motivated and effective approach.
    *   The created datasets (EVOREFUSE-TEST and EVOREFUSE-ALIGN) are valuable contributions and are shown to outperform existing alternatives.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive, evaluating the approach on multiple LLMs and across different dimensions (refusal rate, diversity, confidence, safety).
    *   The analysis of the causes of over-refusal provides insights into model behavior.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a white-box setting is a limitation. EVOREFUSE requires access to the internal workings of the target LLM for calculating the ELBO. This restricts its applicability to closed-source models or proprietary settings.
    *   The dependency on a separate, powerful LLM (GPT-40) for mutation, recombination, and safety filtering introduces a potential bias and increases computational cost.
    *   While the analysis of over-refusal causes is valuable, the findings (shortcut learning and early-layer biases) are not entirely surprising and build upon existing research in the field.  The novelty on the analysis could have been significantly improved.
    *   The paper could strengthen its discussion of failure cases and limitations of the method to build reader confidence in the work.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a more effective benchmark for over-refusal evaluation and a dataset for mitigating this issue through alignment. The insights into over-refusal causes could inform future safety alignment strategies. It could spur future research on over-refusal mitigation and prompt engineering that considers the biases and limitations identified.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**
The paper presents a significant contribution to the field by addressing the practically important problem of over-refusal in LLMs. The EVOREFUSE framework is novel and effective, and the generated datasets are valuable resources. However, the reliance on a white-box setting and the dependency on a separate, powerful LLM are limitations. The insights into over-refusal causes, while valuable, are incremental. While the paper offers compelling results and insightful analysis, the mentioned limitations mean that the assigned score is lower than it could have been. With stronger novelty in the analysis and removal of the white-box constraints, a much stronger score can be achieved.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
